from 20 minutes to two</b></li><li><b>Big savings result for credit unions: $250,000 in the first year at the anonymous credit union</b></li></ul><p><br/></p><p><b>There are still more plusses ticked off by Klacko in the show.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Skeptical?  Also on the show is</b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/kristin-p-vanbeek-065aaa5/'><b> Kris VanBeek</b></a><b>, now CEO at Massachusetts based Rockland Federal Credit Union, soon to be renamed Arise Financial as of August 1.  Rockland has been using Kintera tools for some months and VanBeek sings the praises of these AI tools - and he also talks about the reactions of employees as they put AI into action.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Understand it’s considered rude to ask a credit union exec if his institution is the anonymous one and of course I didn’t ask.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>For the record, Arise Financial (nee Rockland Federal Credit Union) is a $3.7 billion institution.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Longtime listeners will recall that VanBeek is a past guest.  He was in </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-series-172/'><b>episode 172</b></a><b> in 2021 talking about </b><a href='https://www.dorafi.com/'><b>Bank Dora</b></a><b>, an innovative neo bank created by USAlliance, where VanBeek was CEO, and three other institutions.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>I will admit it, when I first heard about BNPL - buy now pay later - I snorted that layaway had died years ago.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>I was wrong.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>BNPL has become a huge marketplace.  Federal Reserve data pegs it at around $160 billion in consumer credit products. And the scary bit for credit unions is that fintechs own over 90% of that market and they aren’t sharing with FIs.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Enter Equipifi which has as its mission empowering credit unions and community banks to offer BNPL to their members and customers.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Equipifi founder Bryce Deeney was on the </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-series-179/'><b>show </b></a><b>five years ago and again </b><a href='https://open.spotify.com/episode/6VEQQxrTOLwtypRgAZK3Ca'><b>three years ago</b></a><b> to discuss the market.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>That market keeps growing and more credit unions are recognizing that BNPL just may appeal to a cohort of their membership -- and it may also attract new members.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Flashforward and on the show now is</b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/lauratrujillo09/'><b> Lo Smith</b></a><b>, a </b><a href='https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/episodes/10227472-cu-2-0-podcast-episode-192-laura-lo-trujillo-array-on-credit-scores-and-your-members'><b>past podcast guest</b></a><b> when she worked at a different company.     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Agentic AI is coming to credit unions and </b><a href='https://saris.ai/'><b>Saris AI</b></a><b> is helping lead the charge.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>What is Agentic AI? Agentic AI acts as an </b><b><em>agent</em></b><b> that autonomously executes multi-step workflows.  That is, Agentic AI does.  It doesn’t tell you what to do.  It does it for you.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Saris AI says it can automate 70% of tedious, complex back office tasks.  It delivers a 300% productivity increase. And 9.8x cost reduction.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>A core focus is lending, but Saris AI also helps with other areas of credit union operations that would benefit from automation.     <itunes:title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 416 Constant AI&#39;s Catherine York Powers on Why You Need to Offer Skip a Pay</itunes:title>
    <title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 416 Constant AI&#39;s Catherine York Powers on Why You Need to Offer Skip a Pay</title>
    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Send us Fan Mail Call this a case of two scenarios. Here’s the background: a member calls and requests to skip a payment on a loan balance.  The loan term will simply extend by a month, interest continues to be collected, and there’s a fee - usually between $15 and $60 - for setting up the skipped payment.   In scenario 1 the customer service rep fields the call, talks with the member, checks to see if the member’s request complies with the institution's guidelines, and also checks to se...]]></itunes:summary>
    <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Call this a case of two scenarios. Here’s the background: a member calls and requests to skip a payment on a loan balance.  The loan term will simply extend by a month, interest continues to be collected, and there’s a fee - usually between $15 and $60 - for setting up the skipped payment.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In scenario 1 the customer service rep fields the call, talks with the member, checks to see if the member’s request complies with the institution&apos;s guidelines, and also checks to see if there are issues that disqualify this member from skipping a payment.  If all’s a go, the rep takes the request to a manager, and the request then circulates up the ladder until it lands on the CEO’s desk for final approval.  That’s for, say, a $200 loan skip.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In scenario 2, an agentic AI bot talks with the member, swiftly computes the eligibility, and if it’s a go, the request is approved.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Which scenario do you like?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Know that in many credit unions scenario 1 prevails and don’t even attempt to calculate the cost of approving that skip a pay request because, often, it costs more than the amount skipped. Many institutions lose money every time they approve a skip a pay request.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Of course enabling skip a pay is a genuine credit union action. This is helping the members who really need it.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Very few banks offer skip a pay because of the costs and the hassle.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>But more credit unions now are offering and one reason why - in addition to the affordability issues confronting many households - is that Maine based fintech </b><a href='https://constant.ai/'><b>Constant AI </b></a><b>has rolled out an agent that already is at work in dozens of credit unions including $8.5 billion Michigan State University Federal Credit Union.  And institutions as small as $100 million in assets also deploy it.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show is Constant AI CEO and founder </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/catherineyork/'><b>Catherine York Powers</b></a><b>, a native Mainer who, when </b><a href='https://constant.ai/about'><b>Powers </b></a><b>finally could, moved her company to her home state and that is why Constant AI is based there.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing? Find out how you can help sponsor this podcast here. Very affordable sponsorship packages are available. Email rjmcgarvey@gmail.com<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>And like this podcast on whatever service you use to stream it. That matters.<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>Find out more about </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/'><b>CU2.0 a</b></a><b>nd the digital transformation of credit unions here. It&apos;s a journey every credit union needs to take. Pronto</b></p>]]></description>

These are not payday loans.  Interest rates are capped at 18% per NCUA policies.   How can this be?  On the show is Scott Patterson, ceo of Student Choice, and the episode starts with a view into the current state of student loans and the big changes that are transf...]]></itunes:summary>
    <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>The Student Choice CUSO, built around student loans, is expanding and now has introduced a program that will enable credit unions to issue small dollar, emergency loans to members with no credit check.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>These are not payday loans.  Interest rates are capped at 18% per NCUA policies.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>How can this be?  On the show is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/scott-patterson-2853941/'><b>Scott Patterson</b></a><b>, ceo of Student Choice, and the episode starts with a view into the current state of student loans and the big changes that are transforming the market.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>But Patterson came on the show to talk up the new</b><a href='https://www.cuinsight.com/press-release/student-choice-launches-employer-choice-to-boost-the-financial-resilience-of-working-americans/'><b> Employer Choice</b></a><b> program that will enable credit unions to make small dollar loans to employees of SEGs and to do so with little risk.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>What kind of voodoo is this? Patterson explains and it’s definitely not voodoo.The key is that the lending is tied into the employee’s payroll system.  Yes, there is no credit check but “instead [there are] employment-based underwriting factors, including job tenure, income stability, and employer characteristics.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Patterson elaborates on this in the show.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The first credit union up with Employer Choice is Affinity Plus Credit Union in Minnesota which, Patterson indicated,has started by offering the program to its own employees.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Other credit unions, says Patterson, want to start by offering the program to SEGs who will set this up as an employee benefit.  So, says Patterson, this has the potential to become a new member acquisition tool.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Remember this: estimates are that half of American families do not have $1000 on hand for emergencies.  A friction-free emergency loan program just might be the life raft many families need and, says Patterson, credit unions can play a big part in helping out those families in need - and doing so at small risk and also low interest rates.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Is the country finally ready for a safe banking act that in effect legalizes cannabis businesses at the federal level?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In recent years multiple bills doing essentially that were passed by the House of Representatives only to be buried in a Senate committee that never reported it out for a floor vote.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>There’s a new House bill in play and this time may be the charm, says Terry Mendez, CEO of Safe Harbor and a past </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-episode-387/'><b>podcast guest</b></a><b>.  Safe Harbor has provided the tools credit unions to offer cannabis banking services since it opened in 2015.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>According to Safe Harbor, the current bill would:</b></p><p><br/></p><ul><li><b>Bar federal banking regulators from prohibiting or discouraging banks from serving state-legal cannabis businesses</b></li><li><b>Bar regulators from terminating or limiting a bank&apos;s deposit insurance because it serves a cannabis client</b></li><li><b>Create a safe harbor from criminal prosecution, liability, and asset forfeiture for banks and their officers/employees serving legitimate state-sanctioned cannabis businesses, while preserving banks&apos; right to decline that business</b></li><li><b>Extend protection to ancillary businesses serving cannabis operators, including lawyers, landlords, and vendors</b></li></ul><p><b><br/></b><br/></p><p><b>By the way, the American Banking Association has come out in support of the act.  In a </b><a href='https://www.marijuanamoment.net/major-banking-group-pushes-congress-to-pass-bill-easing-marijuana-businesses-access-to-financial-services/'><b>letter </b></a><b>to Senate leaders ABA said the bill would “provide important legal and regulatory clarity” and “address a significant challenge facing American businesses, our communities, and the banks that serve them.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show Mendez elaborates on why he believes the bill has a good chance of becoming law.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Can a credit union be a credit union without human employees?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Are credit unions, in the age of AI, at risk of losing the humanity that distinguishes them?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In this provocative episode, keynote speaker </b><a href='https://joshuamevans.com/'><b>Joshua Evans</b></a><b>, who bills himself as an expert on the workplace of the future, talks about how AI is transforming credit unions, how parts of this are to the good, but, he insists, credit unions need to maintain their humanity.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>That’s a lot to think about but it’s thinking that needs doing today as - plainly - AI is transforming many workplaces, credit unions included.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>How often did you use AI yesterday?  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>When a member contacts your credit union is he/she greeted by a human - or an AI bot?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Incidentally, Google Gemini tells me that a credit union needs humans on its board.  That’s non negotiable.  An LLM or AI bot cannot be named to a board seat.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And Gemini raises a philosophical topic: “The credit union movement is built on the ‘People Helping People’  philosophy. A 100% automated entity would likely struggle to maintain its tax-exempt status or its identity as a ‘cooperative’ if there is no human community to cooperate.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Can bill pay be revived at credit unions?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>You may remember, perhaps a generation ago, when bill pay was revered by financial institutions because it was viewed as creating stickier members who, having inputted a dozen or two bill pay recipients, were reluctant to change FIs because of the work involved.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Bill pay lost a lot of its stick when various fintechs rolled out widgets that collected billpay data so it could be transferred to another institution.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The death blow came when many vendors - including most credit card companies - created auto pay tools where a user can choose to pay the minimum or the balance due or amounts in between automatically.  That’s what I use to pay all my recurring bills and the closest I’ve come to using oldfashioned bill pay is when I’ve paid contractors with Zelle a couple times.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/carystrange/'><b>Cary Strange</b></a><b> is on the show to tell how </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/company/payonward/'><b>PayOnward</b></a><b> plans to change that current practice and, hold on, know that PayOnward is a portfolio company of </b><a href='https://blackdragoncap.com/portfolio-companies/payonward/'><b>Black Dragon Capital </b></a><b>which talks about PayOnward this way: “PayOnward is a cloud-native universal payments platform that empowers Community Based Financial Institutions to offer a full suite of AI driven digital payment solutions for both business and consumers through an easy to use and efficient integrated solution.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Now know that Black Dragon Capital  is a private equity firm founded by </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/louishernandezjr/'><b>Louis Hernandez, Jr</b></a><b>. a dozen years ago.  He’s a longtime credit union tech innovator - think the DNA core system.     <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Send us Fan Mail The CUInsight headline caught my eye: “Why credit unions must step up as financial stabilizers to rebuild consumer confidence.”     The piece was authored by  Dave Buerger, CEO of Union Credit and, before that, he was CEO of CuneXus, where he was a co-founder.     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>The CUInsight headline caught my eye: “Why credit unions must step up as financial stabilizers to rebuild consumer confidence.”  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The </b><a href='https://www.cuinsight.com/why-credit-unions-must-step-up-as-financial-stabilizers-to-rebuild-consumer-confidence/'><b>piece </b></a><b>was authored by  Dave Buerger, CEO of Union Credit and, before that, he was CEO of CuneXus, where he was a co-founder.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>At Union Credit the mission is delivering 1-click, perpetual loans to credit union members. 70+ credit unions are Union Credit customers and a key to Union Credit’s business is that it memberizes non members in a very few minutes.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>What triggered Buerger’s piece is a consumer confidence survey commissioned by Union Credit that found that consumer confidence is shaky indeed.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Take aways from the </b><a href='https://www.unioncredit.app/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/2025-Union-Credit-Consumer-Study.pdf'><b>survey </b></a><b>are: “Only 12.5% feel very confident about their finances. The majority are in the middle with 57% rating themselves a 3 or 4 out of 5…. 54% are feeling cautious, 45% anxious, and 30% overwhelmed.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>No wonder many institutions are experiencing a slowing loan volume.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger believes credit unions can take steps to  strengthen consumer confidence, </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger wrote in CUInsight: “Consumers are pressing pause, not stop. They’re delaying major financial decisions because they don’t feel safe committing before they understand their options. Yet 36% told us they would still move forward with financing if the offer felt secure and transparent. That gap between hesitation and action is where credit unions have both a responsibility and an opportunity.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show today he elaborates on these thoughts  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger is optimistic but realistic as well.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And he firmly believes that Union Credit’s tools can help credit unions improve their loan volumes,</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing? Find out how you can help sponsor this podcast here. Very affordable sponsorship packages are available. Email rjmcgarvey@gmail.com<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>And like this podcast on whatever service you use to stream it.     <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>The CUInsight headline caught my eye: “Why credit unions must step up as financial stabilizers to rebuild consumer confidence.”  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The </b><a href='https://www.cuinsight.com/why-credit-unions-must-step-up-as-financial-stabilizers-to-rebuild-consumer-confidence/'><b>piece </b></a><b>was authored by  Dave Buerger, CEO of Union Credit and, before that, he was CEO of CuneXus, where he was a co-founder.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>At Union Credit the mission is delivering 1-click, perpetual loans to credit union members. 70+ credit unions are Union Credit customers and a key to Union Credit’s business is that it memberizes non members in a very few minutes.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>What triggered Buerger’s piece is a consumer confidence survey commissioned by Union Credit that found that consumer confidence is shaky indeed.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Take aways from the </b><a href='https://www.unioncredit.app/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/2025-Union-Credit-Consumer-Study.pdf'><b>survey </b></a><b>are: “Only 12.5% feel very confident about their finances. The majority are in the middle with 57% rating themselves a 3 or 4 out of 5…. 54% are feeling cautious, 45% anxious, and 30% overwhelmed.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>No wonder many institutions are experiencing a slowing loan volume.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger believes credit unions can take steps to  strengthen consumer confidence, </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger wrote in CUInsight: “Consumers are pressing pause, not stop. They’re delaying major financial decisions because they don’t feel safe committing before they understand their options. Yet 36% told us they would still move forward with financing if the offer felt secure and transparent. That gap between hesitation and action is where credit unions have both a responsibility and an opportunity.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show today he elaborates on these thoughts  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Buerger is optimistic but realistic as well.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And he firmly believes that Union Credit’s tools can help credit unions improve their loan volumes,</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>And First Entertainment Credit Union Begat </b><a href='https://finance.yahoo.com/news/first-entertainment-credit-union-nymbus-152100964.html'><b>CineFi</b></a><b>.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Say what?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>First Entertainment is the big show business credit union.  Based in Hollywood, it has assets of around $2.1 billion and serves around 90,000 members, many of them who work in Hollywood, doing everything from acting to the skilled crafts (such as set building) that create Hollywood’s magic.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show is Stephen Owen, CEO of First Entertainment and he is here to tell what CineFi is, why First Entertainment birthed it, and why it is based in Atlanta, GA, some 2300 miles removed from Hollywood.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>CinedFi is a digital only institution, no branches, and right now its offerings consist of checking and savings, although Owen says the plan is to augment that product slate later in 2026.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>How to become a CineFi member? The FOM consists of a handful of Georgia counties, centered around Atlanta, and although the aim is to serve the large number of entertainment industry professionals who live in the Atlanta area,CineFi is open to others as well. Owen explains in the show.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Understand, CineFi does not have its own charter.  Owen tells how it came into existence - and he adds that there may be CineFis opening elsewhere in the country.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Ours is an age when the creation of new credit unions is rare indeed.     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Are you still relying on product based rewards for members?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Meet the new tool on the block: relationship building.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>A trigger is that interchange rates are under attack and, for many financial institutions, it’s been interchange that funded the rewards programs.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>So what’s the new approach? On the show is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/bethmccoy/?originalSubdomain=ca'><b>Beth McCoy</b></a><b>, CEO of </b><a href='https://www.coraloyalty.com/'><b>CORA Loyalty</b></a><b>, who provides a roadmap for building member relationships that benefit both the member and the institution.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>CORA Loyalty explains what it does this way: “Loyalty leaders are shifting from card-centric to relationship-focused campaigns. The approach rewards customers for activities across the institution: maintaining checking accounts, paying account fees, using credit and debit cards, transacting regularly, and long-term relationships.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>“The goal isn’t card spend, but broader and longer lasting outcomes like growing deposits, encouraging treasury management adoption, and strengthening the full banking relationship.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Sound good?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Are you still relying on product based rewards for members?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Meet the new tool on the block: relationship building.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>A trigger is that interchange rates are under attack and, for many financial institutions, it’s been interchange that funded the rewards programs.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>So what’s the new approach? On the show is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/bethmccoy/?originalSubdomain=ca'><b>Beth McCoy</b></a><b>, CEO of </b><a href='https://www.coraloyalty.com/'><b>CORA Loyalty</b></a><b>, who provides a roadmap for building member relationships that benefit both the member and the institution.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>CORA Loyalty explains what it does this way: “Loyalty leaders are shifting from card-centric to relationship-focused campaigns. The approach rewards customers for activities across the institution: maintaining checking accounts, paying account fees, using credit and debit cards, transacting regularly, and long-term relationships.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>“The goal isn’t card spend, but broader and longer lasting outcomes like growing deposits, encouraging treasury management adoption, and strengthening the full banking relationship.”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Sound good?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Fact: credit unions have lots of data about their members. Everything from home address to their liquidity.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Fact: credit unions, most of them, are woefully inept at mining this data to better serve those members and also to boost member retention and up cashflow into the credit union.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Enter </b><a href='https://www.deeptarget.com/'><b>DeepTarget,</b></a><b> a Huntsville AL based fintech that serves 285 clients, 85% of which are credit unions and they range in size from around $25 million in assets to several billion dollars in assets.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>All come to DeepTarget for its toolkit of proven tools that get results. In 2025 for instance DeepTarget tools helped its customers open 328,000 new accounts, generated $1.98 billion in new loans, and brought in $2.7 billion in new deposits.</b></p><p>The DeepTarget secret is how it effectively mines the data a credit union already has on hand.</p><p><b>That sound you hear is the CU cash register ringing.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Monthly DeepTarget fees are affordable, too, typically four figures a month.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And usually a credit union’s own staff involvement in DeepTarget campaigns is minimal, although accommodations are available for credit unions that want a more hands-on role.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In an era of generative AI all this may seem unglamorous and maybe it is.  But it delivers meaningful bottomline results to credit unions.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show is Preetha Pulusani, the company founder and CEO.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And if you wonder how all this tech happens in Huntsville, I have one name for you Wernher von Braun.  If the name doesn’t ring a bell, look it up.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up</b></p><p><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>The Credit Union Generation Barrier.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show today is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/mkrut/'><b>Melissa Krut</b></a><b>, VP of Success at Sogolytics, a Herndon Virginia based firm that focuses on data driven analytics that answer questions such as how satisfied are a credit union’s employees, how satisfied are members, and more.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Krut is here to discuss Sogolytics data driven approach and also to elaborate on a recent Sogolytics </b><a href='https://www.prweb.com/releases/new-sogolytics-study-reveals-growing-disconnect-between-younger-consumers-and-credit-unions-302566658.html#:~:text=A%20new%20Sogolytics%20study%20reveals,Values%20Disconnect'><b>report </b></a><b>that documents the disconnect between younger Millennials and Gen Z and credit unions.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>That’s a disconnect that couldn’t happen at a worse time for credit unions as their Baby Boomer base erodes in numbers.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Here’s one finding: “Nearly half (49%) of Gen Z say they&apos;ve heard of credit unions but don&apos;t understand how they work. Perceptions of being outdated persist: 17% view credit unions as &quot;for older people,&quot; while another 17% believe they are &quot;not modern enough.&quot;”</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Ouch</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>There’s lots of data in the report but, Krut stresses, the data also open doors to better communicating to Millennials and Gen Z.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>By no means is this a battle lost.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And Sogolytics offers plenty of pointers about what  a credit union can do to better appeal to younger potential members.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>I fancy myself something of a minor expert on Medicare and often have advised friends who are approaching 65 about their best options.  Until a man I know approached me and asked for my advice.  He added he was dually qualified for Medicare and Medicaid and he thought he probably qualified for food benefits, too.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Huh?  I had no idea what he was talking about and had to admit to him I was useless in this case.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Today I advised him to find a credit union he could join that also is a customer of Starlight, a fintech with a focus on unlocking some $100 billion in government benefits that are potentially available to credit unions.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Starlight was </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-episode-315/'><b>on the show a year ago</b></a><b>. It’s on again today because it comes with big news: it’s now a CUSO and has won funding from </b><a href='https://www.owfcuso.com/'><b>One Washington Financial</b></a><b>, a CUSO that is wholly owned by </b><a href='https://wsecu.org/'><b>Washington State Employees Credit Union</b></a><b>, the same credit union that  birthed</b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-episode-246/'><b> Q Cash</b></a><b>.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Scott Daukas, a principal at One Washington Financial is on the show to talk about the CUSO and, specifically, why it funded Starlight. He also briefly talks about Silvur, a portfolio company that also has been on the </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-episode-300/'><b>show</b></a><b>.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And Starlight CEO</b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/shreenathr/'><b> Shreenath Regunathan</b></a><b> s back to talk about how it is helping automate the often bewildering process of applying for government benefits - a process made all the more bewildering by massive changes that several big programs (such as SNAP, the food stamps program) are undergoing as the year ends.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Starlight is deployed at over a dozen credit unions and it is busy signing up more credit unions because frankly the need is there.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Along the way, there’s a discussion about Prizeout, an innovative ad tech company that also has been a podcast </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-episode-239/'><b>guest</b></a><b>.  In another </b><a href='https://www.buzzsprout.com/admin/268738/episodes/17654902-cu-2-0-podcast-episode-366-hear-a-credit-union-member-tell-how-her-credit-union-makes-her-life-better'><b>episode </b></a><b>a Prizeout consumer user sings its praises.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>There’s rich content in this episode. Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><br/></p>]]></description>

    <itunes:title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 383 MBFS&#39;s Mark Ritter on Member Business Lending Now</itunes:title>
    <title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 383 MBFS&#39;s Mark Ritter on Member Business Lending Now</title>
    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Send us Fan Mail Mark Ritter, CEO of Member Business Financial Services, a Pennsylvania based CUSO, estimates that about 800 US credit unions do member business lending, meaning that perhaps 3700 don’t.   Many, many more could.  That is spelled out in HR 1151, the law that lets credit unions serve more than one employer group.  Passage of that law is detailed in CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 51 with Marc Schaefer, then the CEO of Truliant CU.   If credit unions can make small business loan...]]></itunes:summary>
    <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Mark </b><a href='https://markritter.com/'><b>Ritter</b></a><b>, CEO of Member Business Financial Services, a Pennsylvania based CUSO, estimates that about 800 US credit unions do member business lending, meaning that perhaps 3700 don’t.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Many, many more could.  That is spelled out in HR 1151, the law that lets credit unions serve more than one employer group.  Passage of that law is detailed in</b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-series-51/#:~:text=So%20how%20is%20it%20now%20legal?%20Listen,the%20legislation%20that%20made%20multiple%20SEGs%20legal.'><b> CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 51</b></a><b> with Marc Schaefer, then the CEO of Truliant CU.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>If credit unions can make small business loans, why don’t they - that’s a question Ritter asks and answers in this show.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>But it also is very clear that he believes credit unions - and their members and communities - would be better served if more credit unions jumped into member business lending. The need on the part of small businesses is acute, many are turning to non traditional lenders who may charge as much as 40% APR.  Credit unions can play an important role in helping their communities&apos; small businesses, says Ritter.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Understand, too, that a CUSO like MBFS - and there are several competitors in the space - may source the loan, it will underwrite it, MBFS will say if it thinks the loan is a definite “yes,” a definite “no,” or - most likely - a maybe.  And if it’s a maybe MBFS will relate the pros and cons of issuing the loan.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The credit union provides the loan capital.  MBFS does the heavy lifting.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show Ritter offers his unfiltered outlook for commercial real estate loans and also predicts a bump in delinquency rates - and yet he is bullish about member business lending in general.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Ritter, by the way, has his own </b><a href='https://markritter.com/podcasts/'><b>podcast</b></a><b>.  Tune in for deep dives in member business trending.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><b><br/>Like what you are hearing? Find out how you can help sponsor this podcast here. Very affordable sponsorship packages are available. Email rjmcgarvey@gmail.com<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>And like this podcast on whatever service you use to stream it. That matters.<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>Find out more about </b><a href='https://cu-2.com/'><b>CU2.0 a</b></a><b>nd the digital transformation of credit unions here. It&apos;s a journey every credit union needs to take. Pronto</b></p><p><br/><br/></p>]]></description>
    <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>Mark </b><a href='https://markritter.com/'><b>Ritter</b></a><b>, CEO of Member Business Financial Services, a Pennsylvania based CUSO, estimates that about 800 US credit unions do member business lending, meaning that perhaps 3700 don’t.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Many, many more could.  That is spelled out in HR 1151, the law that lets credit unions serve more than one employer group.  Passage of that law is detailed in</b><a href='https://cu-2.com/cu-2-0-podcast-series-51/#:~:text=So%20how%20is%20it%20now%20legal?%20Listen,the%20legislation%20that%20made%20multiple%20SEGs%20legal.'><b> CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 51</b></a><b> with Marc Schaefer, then the CEO of Truliant CU.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>If credit unions can make small business loans, why don’t they - that’s a question Ritter asks and answers in this show.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>But it also is very clear that he believes credit unions - and their members and communities - would be better served if more credit unions jumped into member business lending. The need on the part of small businesses is acute, many are turning to non traditional lenders who may charge as much as 40% APR.  Credit unions can play an important role in helping their communities&apos; small businesses, says Ritter.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Understand, too, that a CUSO like MBFS - and there are several competitors in the space - may source the loan, it will underwrite it, MBFS will say if it thinks the loan is a definite “yes,” a definite “no,” or - most likely - a maybe.  And if it’s a maybe MBFS will relate the pros and cons of issuing the loan.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The credit union provides the loan capital.  MBFS does the heavy lifting.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show Ritter offers his unfiltered outlook for commercial real estate loans and also predicts a bump in delinquency rates - and yet he is bullish about member business lending in general.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Ritter, by the way, has his own </b><a href='https://markritter.com/podcasts/'><b>podcast</b></a><b>.  Tune in for deep dives in member business trending.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><b><br/>Like what you are hearing?     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>It’s typically the first in depth contact a non member has with a credit union and that non member, increasingly, is seeking to open a new account online.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Good luck with that.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>The brutal fact is that digital account opening tools at most credit unions are inadequate - and an upshot is a stampede of would-be members who simply abandon the process.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>That’s why today’s show features Philip Paul, CEO of Cotribute, a developer of digital member onboarding tools, and Kathy Richardson VP of Digital Products and Services at CPM Federal Credit Union , a South Carolina institution with assets around $650 million.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Here are results touted by CPM after their implementation of Cotribute tools:</b></p><p><br/></p><ul><li><b>32% increase in new accounts opened in just 90 days</b></li><li><b>82% reduction in manual reviews due to automated fraud detection and decisioning</b></li><li><b>Significant time savings for staff, who are now freed from tedious remediation and cleanup </b></li></ul><p><br/></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Sounds good? It gets better. The Cotribute rollout of its tools at CPM was happening so swiftly, the credit union actually asked them to slow down.  I have never before heard that.     <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>In a year about 500 members of First City Credit Union in Pasadena die.  It’s a credit union with 57,000 members.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And what happened after the member’s death?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/saeidkian/'><b>Saeid Kian</b></a><b>, CEO and co-founder of Ribbon, a fintech that has developed a tool kit to help credit unions speed up the process of settling a member’s estate and also - this is huge - helping the credit union retain some of those assets on its books.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Also on the show is</b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/nav-khanna-02294a4/'><b> Nav Khanna</b></a><b>, CEO of the $900 million First City Credit Union and his is the first credit union to go live with Ribbon.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Why did he agree to be the first? What benefits has First City seen?  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Know this: at most financial institutions the process of handling a deceased member’s accounts is labor intensive.  It annoys the heirs and very likely it also annoys the credit union employees who are tasked with handling the estate.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Saeid knows this first hand because the idea for Ribbon grew out of his own frustrations in settling his father’s estate - a process that took many frustrating weeks.  There had to be a better way, he thought, a way that worked better for the heirs and maybe for the credit union too because the heirs no longer would leave the institution with a settlement check in hand and with the intention to never again deal with that institution.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And Nav tells us it’s really working at First City.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><br/><b>Like what you are hearing? Find out how you can help sponsor this podcast here. Very affordable sponsorship packages are available. Email rjmcgarvey@gmail.com<br/> <br/> </b></p><p><b>And like this podcast on whatever service you use to stream it.     <itunes:title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 365 Derek Coburn on Let&#39;s Retire Retirement</itunes:title>
    <title>CU 2.0 Podcast Episode 365 Derek Coburn on Let&#39;s Retire Retirement</title>
    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Send us Fan Mail When will you retire?   What years of your life should be the ones where you work the longest, hardest hours?   On the show today is Derek Coburn, author of Let's Retire Retirement: How to Enjoy Life to the Fullest—Now and Later, a book that very probably will rock your mind  because it challenges key beliefs that most of us share about retirement.   For instance: at what age will you retire?     Probably your answer is 65, that’s the number that jumps off most lips...]]></itunes:summary>
    <description><![CDATA[<p><a target="_blank" href="https://www.buzzsprout.com/268738/fan_mail/new">Send us Fan Mail</a></p><p><b>When will you retire?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>What years of your life should be the ones where you work the longest, hardest hours?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>On the show today is </b><a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/derekcoburn/'><b>Derek Coburn</b></a><b>, author of </b><a href='https://www.amazon.com/Lets-Retire-Retirement-Enjoy-Fullest-Now-ebook/dp/B0DV7KKLCF/ref=sr_1_1?crid=2FZ0SVM39BXKX&amp;dib=eyJ2IjoiMSJ9.S6R24p_X8fgE-qqGqa98Ljo3oyteNGNUMMsQ-HK32rFllC0vVfjgjgeyvBAVrzSvJL1x55YW4TALpVqHspoYNhlA2FK2OVF50n1_0gpCfVswMcRCNng6X3r03-yECZjA74pzPjH74VqeqT4pTCj1cwC9Otf5mq5g2vJUp0D0k08qLkbI9rt-9NBazSqQ-W1B.lvbDO_zBlyc1eY5xEvjyjeAFvIH9mg_tcR-NbuJRBtc&amp;dib_tag=se&amp;keywords=derek+coburn&amp;qid=1750195915&amp;sprefix=derek+coburn%2Caps%2C161&amp;sr=8-1'><b>Let&apos;s Retire Retirement: How to Enjoy Life to the Fullest—Now and Later</b></a><b>, a book that very probably will rock your mind  because it challenges key beliefs that most of us share about retirement.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>For instance: at what age will you retire?  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Probably your answer is 65, that’s the number that jumps off most lips.  But Coburn says probably you won’t and he adds the zinger that other ages may well work much better for you.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Like what? How about 75?  Or 70?</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And fact is more Americans are working longer- indeed the fastest growing age cohort in the workplace is Americans aged 75 and older!</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Surveys also say that approaching 25% of Americans age 65 and higher are now working.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>And Ciburn believes that number will only increase as many American seniors are today healthier, more active than were Americans of the same age a generation ago.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>A bonus: retire later in life and you need far less retirement savings.  You’ll hear actual numbers in the show - and it will blow your mind.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>As for what years you should work longest and hardest? Coburn argues that it’s when your kids are home and for many that means working less in your thirties and forties.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Is that catastrophic for your retirement savings?  No, because you will be working later in life you need less retirement savings.  QED.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show, mention is made of an app. Death Clock AI.  Here’s a link to the Washington Post </b><a href='https://www.washingtonpost.com/health/2025/06/08/death-clock-ai-life-expectancy/'><b>story </b></a><b>about it.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show Coburn mentions CU 2.0 founder/CEO Kirk Drake and </b><a href='https://www.resistancewineco.com/?srsltid=AfmBOoq3kJgowR4RDP2w2c3RnNjafxX1KclxVsIxU-QaiPreJO7kHqPk'><b>Resistance Wine</b></a><b>.  I’ve drunk it, it’s good.</b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Coburn says he wears an Oura ring. Here’s the </b><a href='https://ouraring.com/?srsltid=AfmBOorpdjTXAvhzcjXJAOX3nNWNCh1vrSTSiFCP6ik0Nk-8PUYwcyvn'><b>link</b></a></p><p><br/></p><p><b>In the show also there’s mention of </b><a href='https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihaly_Csikszentmihalyi'><b>Mihaly Csikszentmihalyi</b></a><b>, a brilliant psychologist who documented the phenomenon he called </b><a href='https://www.amazon.com/Flow-Psychology-Experience-Perennial-Classics-ebook/dp/B000W94FE6/ref=sr_1_1?crid=24JSYBPSEN64C&amp;dib=eyJ2IjoiMSJ9.JAPMeDJM_rIJJcuxRln8TWCh3rTRBcUGoknHQ28EjHiF_jtKiz8tAvn38QWWzDHS2OcTRlJW2hlLhbzxjNsqTMES-B6EvyMJNeD6fVE7k4twyI8A2mgSag7iQlqcpFaLqk2Dz8UraNMlw8Jxlsyqp16Qzf81rxM-2P_8MSyBxTht-bU2A5wY0INYMJrw_wmjHnsNkwkdl-aiXN4KZLdaCVIO5ahv2FC4Sfmjnmmi5IbnSyPZG4PBmfu2-qR-c8ChmtmUskkDwNuLP6-BHpHwHPlp4ek5XxN1SscEScG5zuc.WTG_IV-parWbxqEB1yPdO8UpDVSZ52p_mEmKNc4VaLY&amp;dib_tag=se&amp;keywords=flow&amp;qid=1750197239&amp;sprefix=flow%2Caps%2C231&amp;sr=8-1'><b>flow </b></a><b>where work is highly focused and we are happiest.  </b></p><p><br/></p><p><b>Listen up.</b></p><p><b><br/>Like what you are hearing? Fi</b></p>]]></description>
